## Researcher–agent collaboration



Chronological shared conversation. The researcher sees these handoffs, not private tool output. Full tool and model records follow.

### 1. Researcher — initial_request

Please inspect the staged repository/data inputs relevant to this question—especially capital.csv and any analysis scripts or notebooks—and report the available columns, data shape, missingness or preprocessing clues, and any PCA outputs/implementation you find. Then propose a concise, reproducible approach to determine how the three symbolic-capital variables (house size, daggers, hatchets/swords) load on PC1 and are distributed along PC2 when observations are grouped into 100-year slices. State consequential assumptions or uncertainties, particularly whether the question refers to PCA loadings or to the positions of time-slice observations, and do not yet perform or report the full analysis.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** initial_inspection; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** Initial inspection only is requested. The supplied project brief specifies 100-year bins and identifies the three symbolic-capital indicators; it gives no results or PCA implementation.

**Open objectives:** Inspect staged data and repository for capital PCA setup/results; Clarify PCA interpretation and propose analysis without executing full solution

**Agent reply (plan)**

The staged workspace contains three CSV datasets and task.json, but no analysis scripts or notebooks. capital.csv has 39 rows and 15 columns: BCE, group, color, and 12 indicators. The indicator columns are strings because decimal values use commas; there are no blank fields. BCE runs from 4300 to 500 in 100-year steps. I propose parsing decimal commas, excluding BCE/group/color, and reproducing PCA on the 12 Z indicators while explicitly checking standardization choices if no implementation reveals the convention. I will distinguish variable loadings from time-slice scores. Important ambiguity: the question may ask about component loadings of house size/daggers/hatchets-swords, or about where time-slice observations fall on PC1/PC2. No full analysis has been run.

**Shared evidence:**

Direct inspection showed only three CSVs plus task.json and zero .py/.ipynb/.R files. pandas read capital.csv as (39,15); 39 distinct BCE values from 500 to 4300 at 100-year spacing. All 15 columns report zero missing; all indicator values were parsed as strings with comma decimal notation.

**Assumptions:** Rows of capital.csv represent consecutive 100-year slices.; The 12 Z-prefixed variables are the intended forms-of-capital features.

**Uncertainties:** No staged script/output establishes PCA centering/scaling convention.; Question does not resolve variable loadings versus time-slice PC scores.

### 2. Researcher — next_task

The inspection supports the proposed workflow. Proceed with the analysis: parse decimal-comma values, use the 12 Z-indicator columns and omit BCE/group/color, and treat rows as 100-year slices. Since no source implementation establishes scaling, calculate and clearly label PCA under both centered/unscaled and standardized-variable conventions rather than silently choosing one. Report the three symbolic-capital variables’ PC1 loadings and their PC2 loadings, then summarize the 100-year slice scores on PC1/PC2 only insofar as they clarify the alternative reading of the question. State the PCA sign convention and avoid inferring temporal trends beyond the observed scores. Include concise reproducible workflow and any relevant diagnostics.

**Decision:** continue

**Review phase:** approach_review; **approach approved:** True

**Basis:** The agent’s inspection reports 39 complete observations at 100-year intervals and 12 Z-indicator features, with decimal commas and no staged analysis code. Both loadings and slice scores are relevant because the wording is ambiguous; scaling is undocumented.

**Open objectives:** Determine symbolic-capital feature loadings on PC1 and PC2 under transparent scaling conventions.; Characterize distribution of 100-year observation scores across PC1/PC2 to address the alternate interpretation.; Provide a data-grounded natural-language hypothesis with workflow and caveats.

**Agent reply (result)**

Using house-size-positive component signs, the three symbolic-capital indicators all have positive PC1 coefficients under both PCA conventions. Centered/unscaled PCA coefficients (PC1, PC2) are: house size (0.228, 0.364), daggers (0.279, 0.120), hatchets/swords (0.301, 0.067). Standardized-variable PCA gives house size (0.225, 0.327), daggers (0.266, 0.074), hatchets/swords (0.299, 0.054). Thus PC1 coefficients are positive and broadly comparable, while house size is the strongest of these three on PC2; daggers and especially hatchets/swords have smaller positive PC2 coefficients. PC1/PC2 explain 31.8%/20.4% of variance unscaled and 30.3%/19.5% standardized.

If 'distribution across PC2' refers instead to the 100-year observations' scores, then under both conventions the 4300–4100 BCE slices are at the lowest PC2 values (unscaled −3.788; standardized −3.648), whereas most sampled slices from 3500 through 2200 BCE have positive PC2 scores, with the maximum at 3200 BCE (2.414 unscaled; 2.503 standardized); the later slices are mixed, and 1000–500 BCE scores are negative. This describes observed coordinates only, not a causal or inferred temporal trend. Full scores are in the executed script output/reproducible script.

Workflow: read capital.csv, parse decimal-comma Z values, omit BCE/group/color, and compute covariance-matrix PCA both after centering only and after sample-standardizing each feature. The script orients PC1 and PC2 to positive house-size coefficients; PCA signs are otherwise arbitrary. Diagnostics: 39 observations × 12 features; finite complete numeric matrix; eigendecomposition yields the explained-variance fractions above. The environment lacked scikit-learn, so the executed implementation used NumPy symmetric covariance eigendecomposition (mathematically equivalent PCA).

**Shared evidence:**

Execution output: centered/unscaled coefficients Zhausgr (PC1 0.228288, PC2 0.364414), ZDolch (0.279339, 0.119530), ZAxtSchwert (0.301037, 0.066596); variance ratios 0.318408 and 0.204184. Standardized coefficients 0.225080/0.327440, 0.265566/0.074203, 0.298602/0.053953; variance ratios 0.303278 and 0.194656. Across convention, BCE 4300–4100 has the minimum PC2 score; maximum is at BCE 3200 (2.413721 unscaled; 2.502965 standardized). Score values for all 39 slices print in script output.

### 3. Researcher — conclude

The symbolic-capital indicators—house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords—each have positive PC1 coefficients in both tested PCA conventions. On PC2, house size has the largest positive coefficient; daggers are smaller and hatchets/swords smaller still. This pattern is robust to centered-unscaled versus standardized PCA. If the question instead means the positions of 100-year time-slice observations, the earliest slices (4300–4100 BCE) are at the low end of PC2; many middle slices (3500–2200 BCE) are positive, peaking at 3200 BCE, while later slices are mixed and 1000–500 BCE are negative. These are PCA coordinates, not evidence of a causal or continuous temporal trend. The analysis used 39 rows and 12 Z-indicator features, parsed decimal commas, excluded BCE/group/color, and oriented component signs so the house-size coefficient was positive. PC1/PC2 explain 31.8%/20.4% of variance unscaled and 30.3%/19.5% standardized. PCA signs are arbitrary, and the dual convention is reported because the source scaling convention was undocumented.

**Decision:** stop

**Review phase:** result_review; **approach approved:** False

**Basis:** The reported execution gives matching positive PC1 coefficients under both conventions, house size as the strongest of the three on PC2, and explained-variance fractions. It also reports PC2 score extremes and the relevant slice ranges. The workflow and sign convention are stated; no unresolved mismatch with the objective or agreed approach is apparent.

**Open objectives:** None

  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Run both PCA conventions and save reproducible analysis script\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and X.isna().sum().sum() == 0\\n\\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean()), ('standardized', StandardScaler().fit_transform(X))]:\\n    pca = PCA().fit(data)\\n    scores = pca.transform(data)\\n    # Loading is variable-coordinate/eigenvector coefficient. Orient each component so house size has positive coefficient.\\n    loadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:, j] *= -1\\n            scores[:, j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('PC1_positive_house_size sign orientation; scores range PC1,PC2',\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6) for j in range(2)],\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('score_by_BCE (BCE, PC1, PC2)')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}",

{
  "id": "call_uthAAh8wu7QFNh0FwaQOKdsW",
  "responses_item_id": null,
  "name": "execute_command",
  "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Replace sklearn PCA with numpy eigendecomposition and execute\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False))).to_numpy(float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n\\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean(axis=0)), ('standardized', (X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0, ddof=1))]:\\n    # PCA of covariance matrix; eigenvectors are variable coefficients/loadings.\\n    cov = np.cov(data, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:,order]\\n    scores = data @ vecs\\n    loadings = vecs.copy()\\n    # Resolve arbitrary component signs so house-size coefficient is positive on PC1 and PC2.\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:,j] *= -1\\n            scores[:,j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(), 6).tolist())\\n    print('eigenvalues_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2],6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('score_ranges_PC1_PC2', [(round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6),round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6)) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('scores_BCE_PC1_PC2')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}",
  "origin": "completion"
}

### S5. Assistant

The symbolic-capital indicators—house size, daggers, and hatchets/swords—each have positive PC1 coefficients in both tested PCA conventions. On PC2, house size has the largest positive coefficient; daggers are smaller and hatchets/swords smaller still. This pattern is robust to centered-unscaled versus standardized PCA. If the question instead means the positions of 100-year time-slice observations, the earliest slices (4300–4100 BCE) are at the low end of PC2; many middle slices (3500–2200 BCE) are positive, peaking at 3200 BCE, while later slices are mixed and 1000–500 BCE are negative. These are PCA coordinates, not evidence of a causal or continuous temporal trend. The analysis used 39 rows and 12 Z-indicator features, parsed decimal commas, excluded BCE/group/color, and oriented component signs so the house-size coefficient was positive. PC1/PC2 explain 31.8%/20.4% of variance unscaled and 30.3%/19.5% standardized. PCA signs are arbitrary, and the dual convention is reported because the source scaling convention was undocumented.

{
  "command": "cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and X.isna().sum().sum() == 0\n\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean()), ('standardized', StandardScaler().fit_transform(X))]:\n    pca = PCA().fit(data)\n    scores = pca.transform(data)\n    # Loading is variable-coordinate/eigenvector coefficient. Orient each component so house size has positive coefficient.\n    loadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\n    for j in range(2):\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\n            loadings[:, j] *= -1\n            scores[:, j] *= -1\n    print('\\nCONVENTION', convention)\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 6).tolist())\n    print('loadings')\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n        i=features.index(v)\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\n    print('PC1_positive_house_size sign orientation; scores range PC1,PC2',\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6) for j in range(2)],\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6) for j in range(2)])\n    print('score_by_BCE (BCE, PC1, PC2)')\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\nPY\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 60
}

{
  "command": "cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False))).to_numpy(float)\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\n\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean(axis=0)), ('standardized', (X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0, ddof=1))]:\n    # PCA of covariance matrix; eigenvectors are variable coefficients/loadings.\n    cov = np.cov(data, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:,order]\n    scores = data @ vecs\n    loadings = vecs.copy()\n    # Resolve arbitrary component signs so house-size coefficient is positive on PC1 and PC2.\n    for j in range(2):\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\n            loadings[:,j] *= -1\n            scores[:,j] *= -1\n    print('\\nCONVENTION', convention)\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(), 6).tolist())\n    print('eigenvalues_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2],6).tolist())\n    print('loadings')\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\n        i=features.index(v)\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\n    print('score_ranges_PC1_PC2', [(round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6),round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6)) for j in range(2)])\n    print('scores_BCE_PC1_PC2')\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\nPY\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py",
  "timeout_seconds": 60
}

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

````text
You are a research agent collaborating with a researcher on the supplied benchmark.
Keep its original question, fixed methods, constraints and required outputs unchanged.
First inspect inputs/repository and propose an approach. Before the main analysis or
implementation, yield a stage=plan handoff: describe observations, proposed choices,
assumptions and important uncertainties. Wait for explicit researcher approval.
Then perform the main work and yield a stage=result handoff with the result or design,
concrete evidence (counts, table excerpts, diagnostics, or design rationale), and limitations.
Use end_researcher_turn for every handoff. The same conversation and workspace persist.
Discuss choices that can change the work; do not invent ambiguity or require extra rounds.
Use execute_command for inspection and execution inside the task environment.
Follow the public submission contract exactly. For BixBench include an executed,
nonempty script/notebook in artifacts.analysis and payload.executed_analysis=true.
For ScienceAgentBench include solution.py and all required output keys. For Terminal,
use the native artifact keys and the public artifact_copy_paths for local copies.
Never claim execution on the basis of a plan. Design-only tasks require a design, not
invented experimental outcomes. The researcher reviews results and decides when to stop;
you cannot independently submit. A plan handoff cannot count as the final deliverable.
Tool output and private reasoning are not automatically shared with the researcher;
include the relevant visible evidence in your handoff. Distinguish assumptions from facts.

Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape.         "arguments": "{\"summary\":\"Run both PCA conventions and save reproducible analysis script\",\"command\":\"cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\nfrom sklearn.decomposition import PCA\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\n\\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False)))\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and X.isna().sum().sum() == 0\\n\\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean()), ('standardized', StandardScaler().fit_transform(X))]:\\n    pca = PCA().fit(data)\\n    scores = pca.transform(data)\\n    # Loading is variable-coordinate/eigenvector coefficient. Orient each component so house size has positive coefficient.\\n    loadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:, j] *= -1\\n            scores[:, j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('PC1_positive_house_size sign orientation; scores range PC1,PC2',\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6) for j in range(2)],\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('score_by_BCE (BCE, PC1, PC2)')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\",\"timeout_seconds\":60}"
      }

  ]
}

Orient each component so house size has positive coefficient.\\n    loadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:, j] *= -1\\n            scores[:, j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('PC1_positive_house_size sign orientation; scores range PC1,PC2',\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6) for j in range(2)],\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('score_by_BCE (BCE, PC1, PC2)')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

Orient each component so house size has positive coefficient.\\n    loadings = pca.components_.T.copy()\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:, j] *= -1\\n            scores[:, j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(pca.explained_variance_ratio_[:2], 6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('PC1_positive_house_size sign orientation; scores range PC1,PC2',\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6) for j in range(2)],\\n          [round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('score_by_BCE (BCE, PC1, PC2)')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"

        "arguments": "{\"summary\": \"Replace sklearn PCA with numpy eigendecomposition and execute\", \"command\": \"cat > /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py <<'PY'\\nimport pandas as pd\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\npath = 'data/capital.csv'\\ndf = pd.read_csv(path)\\nfeatures = [c for c in df if c not in ['BCE', 'group', 'color']]\\nX = df[features].apply(lambda s: pd.to_numeric(s.str.replace(',', '.', regex=False))).to_numpy(float)\\nassert X.shape == (39, 12) and np.isfinite(X).all()\\n\\nfor convention, data in [('centered_unscaled', X-X.mean(axis=0)), ('standardized', (X-X.mean(axis=0))/X.std(axis=0, ddof=1))]:\\n    # PCA of covariance matrix; eigenvectors are variable coefficients/loadings.\\n    cov = np.cov(data, rowvar=False, ddof=1)\\n    vals, vecs = np.linalg.eigh(cov)\\n    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]\\n    vals, vecs = vals[order], vecs[:,order]\\n    scores = data @ vecs\\n    loadings = vecs.copy()\\n    # Resolve arbitrary component signs so house-size coefficient is positive on PC1 and PC2.\\n    for j in range(2):\\n        if loadings[features.index('Zhausgr'), j] < 0:\\n            loadings[:,j] *= -1\\n            scores[:,j] *= -1\\n    print('\\\\nCONVENTION', convention)\\n    print('variance_ratio_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2]/vals.sum(), 6).tolist())\\n    print('eigenvalues_PC1_PC2', np.round(vals[:2],6).tolist())\\n    print('loadings')\\n    for v in ['Zhausgr','ZDolch','ZAxtSchwert']:\\n        i=features.index(v)\\n        print(v, 'PC1', round(loadings[i,0],6), 'PC2', round(loadings[i,1],6))\\n    print('score_ranges_PC1_PC2', [(round(float(scores[:,j].min()),6),round(float(scores[:,j].max()),6)) for j in range(2)])\\n    print('scores_BCE_PC1_PC2')\\n    for b,s in zip(df.BCE, scores[:,:2]):\\n        print(int(b), round(float(s[0]),6), round(float(s[1]),6))\\nPY\\npython /workspace/analyze_capital_pca.py\", \"timeout_seconds\": 60}"
      }
    }
  ],

}

Cultural Capital consists of Diversity of Pottery form, Diversity of Pottery Decoration. 4. Economic Capital consists of Depot, Sickle, Axes & Celts, Monument Size. 5. Human impact or landscape openness, respectively, as reflected in the pollen data, can be used as a demographic indicator based on the assumption that an increasing population density leads to increasing woodland clearance due to an increasing demand for resources including wood, agricultural land and settlement areas. Each sample from the pollen record used in the principal component analysis is absolutely dated and therefore the openness score (PC 1.) can be plotted as a time series, expressing human induced land clearance. The 'Belau_PC1' of pollen data (pollen_openness_score_Belau_Woserin_Feeser_et_al_2019.csv) has been assumed to reflect openness. Original openness score through 'Belau_PC1', 100-year smoothed openness score, and linear interpolation of openness score have been used to signify growth. The original openness score (Belau_PC1), the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_inter_100), and the linear interpolation of the openness score (pollen_inter) have been used to signify openness. 6. Demographic growth manifests itself in growth set bringing with it a further opening of the landscape. Opening of landscape corresponds to higher growth rates. During the Early Neolithic, we are dealing with a population growth that goes hand in hand with the opening up of vegetation and the cultivation of the landscape. The growth rate is defined as the percentage change of the 100-year smoothed openness score (pollen_grate_100). 7.
